```text
312	\subsection{Cross-Scale Full Results}
313	\label{app:cross_scale_full}
314	
315	This section provides the full cross-scale analysis summarized in \S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}.
316	We extend the benchmark to two 7--8B models: Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, both under 4-bit NF4 QLoRA fine-tuning, generating depths $d \in \{0, 1, \ldots, 5\}$ with nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$, 500 samples per depth).
317	Feature extraction uses the same reference model (Pythia-1.4B) and 19-feature pipeline as all prior experiments.
318	
319	\paragraph{Scale comparison.}
320	Table~\ref{tab:cross_scale} contrasts the two 7--8B models with the three 1--1.5B models on matched evaluation (nucleus sampling, depths 0--3).
321	
322	\begin{table}[h]
323	\centering
324	\small
325	\caption{Cross-scale comparison: 4-class RF accuracy (\%, depths 0--3, nucleus), $\mathrm{EDD}_{T{=}0.70}$ (depths 0--5), and d0-vs-d1 AUC. CI: 95\% bootstrap. Chance = 25\%.}
326	\label{tab:cross_scale}
327	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
328	\toprule
329	Model & 4-class acc & $\mathrm{EDD}_{T{=}0.70}$ & d0v1 AUC \\
330	\midrule
331	GPT-2~XL (1.5B) & 83.3 & 4 & 0.999 \\
332	OLMo-1B & 78.8 & 4 & 0.997 \\
333	Pythia-1.4B & 78.2 & 4 & 0.998 \\
334	\midrule
335	Llama-3.1-8B & 70.2\,[68.3, 72.4] & 4 & 0.949 \\
336	Qwen2.5-7B & 60.1\,[58.0, 62.1] & ${>}5$ & 0.969 \\
337	\bottomrule
338	\end{tabular}
339	\end{table}
340	
341	\paragraph{(1) Depth signal persists at 7--8B scale.}
342	Both 7--8B models substantially exceed the 25\% chance baseline: Llama-3.1-8B achieves 70.2\% (95\% CI $[68.3, 72.4]$) and Qwen2.5-7B achieves 60.1\% ($[58.0, 62.1]$), confirming that recursive depth remains estimable from perplexity-based features even when the generator is 5--7$\times$ larger than the reference model.
343	Depth-0 separability is preserved in both models: d0v1 AUC is 0.949 (Llama-3.1) and 0.969 (Qwen2.5).
344	
345	\paragraph{(2) EDD at 7--8B scale.}
346	At threshold $T{=}0.70$, Qwen2.5-7B maintains all consecutive-pair AUCs above 0.70 through depth~5 (Table~\ref{tab:cross_scale_auc}), yielding $\mathrm{EDD}_{T{=}0.70} > 5$, exceeding the $\mathrm{EDD}{=}4$ observed at 1--1.5B.
347	Llama-3.1-8B matches the smaller models at $\mathrm{EDD}{=}4$: its monotonic decay causes d4v5 (0.618) to drop below $T$, despite stronger signal at shallower depths.
348	Both larger generators produce weaker per-step signal than the 1--1.5B models (mean consecutive AUC: 0.786 for Llama-3.1, 0.782 for Qwen2.5, vs.\ 0.852 at 1--1.5B).
349	
350	\begin{table}[h]
351	\centering
352	\small
353	\caption{Consecutive binary AUC for 7--8B models (nucleus $p{=}0.95$). Llama-3.1-8B exhibits monotonic decay (d4v5 drops below $T{=}0.70$); Qwen2.5-7B shows a non-monotonic (U-shaped) pattern with all pairs above $T$.}
354	\label{tab:cross_scale_auc}
355	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccc@{}}
356	\toprule
357	Model & d0v1 & d1v2 & d2v3 & d3v4 & d4v5 \\
358	\midrule
359	Llama-3.1-8B & 0.949 & 0.877 & 0.771 & 0.716 & 0.618 \\
360	Qwen2.5-7B & 0.969 & 0.729 & 0.709 & 0.732 & 0.772 \\
361	\bottomrule
362	\end{tabular}
363	\end{table}
364	
365	\paragraph{(3) Divergent AUC trajectories and instruction-tuning effect.}
366	Among instruction-tuned models, Llama-3.1-8B exhibits monotonic AUC decay consistent with the 1--1.5B pattern, while Qwen2.5-7B shows a U-shaped recovery beyond d2v3 (Table~\ref{tab:cross_scale_auc}).
367	A base-model control (Llama-3.1-8B without instruction tuning) clarifies the causal direction: the base model achieves 59.9\% four-class accuracy (95\% CI [57.8, 62.15], $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$) with non-monotonic AUC decay (Table~\ref{tab:llama3_base_auc}), while the instruction-tuned variant reaches 70.2\% with strictly monotonic decay.
368	The base-model control experiment suggests instruction-tuning regularizes rather than confounds the depth signal.
369	
370	\begin{table}[h]
371	\centering
372	\small
373	\caption{Pairwise AUC: Llama-3.1-8B Base vs.\ Instruct.}
374	\label{tab:llama3_base_auc}
375	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
376	\toprule
377	Variant & $d_{0\text{v}1}$ & $d_{1\text{v}2}$ & $d_{2\text{v}3}$ & $d_{3\text{v}4}$ & $d_{4\text{v}5}$ \\
378	\midrule
379	Base & 0.918 & 0.794 & 0.682 & 0.730 & 0.595 \\
380	Instruct & 0.965 & 0.874 & 0.771 & 0.716 & 0.618 \\
381	\bottomrule
382	\end{tabular}
383	\end{table}
384	
385	The base model exhibits non-monotonic decay with a U-shaped recovery at $d_{3\text{v}4}$, while the instruction-tuned variant shows strictly monotonic decay. Both variants exceed chance (AUC $> 0.5$) at all boundaries, confirming depth signal exists regardless of instruction-tuning, though instruction-tuning strengthens and regularizes it.
386	
387	\paragraph{Caveats.}
388	For Qwen2.5-7B, the CI lower bound (58.0\%) does not exclude sub-60\% accuracy; Llama-3.1-8B's lower bound (68.3\%) is more comfortably above chance but still below the 75--83\% range at 1--1.5B.
389	The divergent AUC trajectories across two models caution against strong generalizations from only two 7--8B data points: both the monotonic (Llama-3.1) and non-monotonic (Qwen2.5) patterns are single observations, and further architectures are needed to establish which is the dominant regime at this scale.
390	
391	\paragraph{Confounds.}
392	\label{app:cross_scale_confounds}
393	Four confounds limit these results: (1)~4-bit NF4 QLoRA quantization (absent at 1--1.5B) may distort the perplexity distribution independently of depth; (2)~smaller sample size (500 vs.\ 5{,}000 per depth) widens confidence intervals; (3)~evaluation is limited to nucleus sampling, leaving other strategies untested at this scale; (4)~vocabulary mismatch between 7--8B generators and the 1.4B reference model.
394	The base-model control partially addresses a former confound (instruction-tuning): since the base variant shows weaker signal with non-monotonic decay while the instruction-tuned variant shows stronger monotonic signal, instruction-tuning regularizes rather than confounds depth estimation.
395	The remaining confounds are not mutually independent (quantization and vocabulary mismatch both affect the perplexity distribution), making it infeasible to attribute the observed accuracy gap (60--70\% vs.\ 78--85\% at 1--1.5B) to any single factor.
396	
397	\subsection{Cross-Boundary Generalization}
398	\label{app:cross_boundary}
399	
400	The preceding experiments assume a single model family generates all recursive depths.
401	In practice, recursive contamination may traverse model boundaries: text generated by model~A is used to train model~B, whose output trains model~C.
402	We probe whether the depth signal persists under two relaxations of the single-model assumption.
403	
404	\paragraph{Cross-model recursive chains.}
405	We construct a heterogeneous chain: human text (d0) $\to$ Pythia-1.4B generates d1 $\to$ OLMo-1B finetuned on Pythia-d1 generates d2 $\to$ GPT-2~XL finetuned on OLMo-d2 generates d3.
406	Each recursive step uses a \emph{different} model family, eliminating the possibility that depth signal arises from a single model's idiosyncratic behavior.
407	A 4-class Random Forest achieves 84.75\% accuracy (95\% CI $[81.0, 88.25]$), with per-depth F1 scores of 0.98 (d0), 0.88 (d1), 0.76 (d2), and 0.76 (d3).
408	Pairwise AUC remains high: d0-vs-d1 = 0.999 and d2-vs-d3 = 0.841.
409	These results suggest that distributional artifacts of recursive generation persist across model boundaries, providing preliminary evidence consistent with a process-level depth signal rather than individual generator artifacts.
410	
411	\paragraph{Confound discussion.}
412	The heterogeneous chain confounds architecture identity with depth (d1=Pythia, d2=OLMo, d3=GPT-2~XL).
413	However, if the classifier exploited architecture fingerprints rather than depth signal, near-perfect accuracy would be expected since architecture perfectly determines depth label.
414	The observed 84.75\% with monotonically decreasing per-depth F1 (d0=0.98, d1=0.88, d2=0.76, d3=0.76) is more consistent with a depth-signal interpretation, as architecture fingerprinting would not produce this graded pattern.
415	We address this confound directly with a reverse-chain control below.
416	
417	\paragraph{Reverse-chain control.}
418	To empirically test the architecture confound hypothesis, we constructed a reverse chain (GPT-2~XL $\to$ OLMo $\to$ Pythia) reversing the model-depth assignment.
419	The reverse chain achieves 85.75\% (95\% CI [82.0, 89.25]), statistically indistinguishable from the forward chain (84.75\%, [81.0, 88.25]).
420	If the classifier exploited architecture fingerprints rather than depth signal, reversing the chain should invert depth predictions; the equivalent accuracy under both orderings provides empirical evidence against the architecture confound.
421	
422	\paragraph{Cross-domain transfer.}
423	All prior experiments use WikiText-103 as the seed corpus.
424	To test domain dependence, we repeat the pipeline with arXiv abstracts as the seed text (Pythia-1.4B, nucleus $p{=}0.95$, 500 samples per depth, d0--d3).
425	Five-fold cross-validation yields 74.1\% $\pm$ 2.0\% accuracy, compared to ${\sim}$78\% on the WikiText seed.
426	Per-depth F1 shows the familiar pattern: d0 = 1.00, d1 = 0.78, d2 = 0.52, d3 = 0.66, with d2 remaining the most challenging class.
427	The 4pp accuracy gap relative to WikiText likely reflects the narrower stylistic range of scientific abstracts, which may reduce the distributional contrast between adjacent depths.
428	These results provide preliminary evidence that the perplexity-based features capture properties of the recursive generation process rather than domain-specific artifacts of the seed corpus.
429	
430	\subsection{Cross-Seed Replication}
431	\label{app:seed_replication}
432	
433	Table~\ref{tab:seed_replication} reports within-seed and cross-seed classification accuracy for Pythia-1.4B (nucleus, depths 0--3) under two independent LoRA training seeds.
434	
435	\begin{table}[h]
436	\centering
437	\small
438	\caption{Cross-seed transfer (Pythia-1.4B nucleus). Baseline seed=42 is the primary benchmark LoRA; seed=43 is an independent replication. $\Delta$: difference from respective comparison point.}
439	\label{tab:seed_replication}
440	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
441	\toprule
442	Condition & Acc (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
443	\midrule
444	Within-seed (seed=42) & 78.20 & --- \\
445	Within-seed (seed=43) & 77.15 & $-$1.05$^\dagger$ \\
446	\midrule
447	Cross-seed (train 42$\to$test 43) & 77.85 & \\
448	Cross-seed (train 43$\to$test 42) & 75.68 & \\
449	Cross-seed avg & 76.76 & $-$1.30$^\ddagger$ \\
450	\bottomrule
451	\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\footnotesize $^\dagger$vs.\ baseline (78.20\%). $^\ddagger$vs.\ within-seed avg (78.07\%).}
452	\end{tabular}
453	\end{table}
454	
455	The seed=43 LoRA achieves 77.15\% (95\% CI [76.4, 78.15]), within 1.05\,pp of the seed=42 baseline.
456	Cross-seed transfer averages 76.76\%, a 1.30\,pp deficit relative to within-seed performance.
457	Top-5 feature importance is identical across seeds (\texttt{var\_surprisal}, \texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{mean\_ppl}, \texttt{var\_ppl}, \texttt{p75\_ppl}), confirming that depth signal arises from the recursive process itself rather than idiosyncratic LoRA weight configurations.
458	
459	\subsection{Generation Length Ablation}
460	\label{app:length_ablation}
461	
462	Table~\ref{tab:length_ablation} reports classification accuracy as a function of passage length (Pythia-1.4B nucleus, depths 0--3).
463	
464	\begin{table}[h]
465	\centering
466	\small
467	\caption{Generation length ablation (Pythia-1.4B nucleus, 5-fold CV). Accuracy increases monotonically with passage length; the 9.47\,pp spread confirms that longer passages provide stronger statistical signal.}
468	\label{tab:length_ablation}
469	\begin{tabular}{@{}lc@{}}
470	\toprule
471	Passage length (tokens) & Acc (\%) \\
472	\midrule
473	128 & 68.35 \\
474	256 & 73.80 \\
475	512 & 77.82 \\
476	\bottomrule
477	\end{tabular}
478	\end{table}
479	
480	Depth signal is detectable from 128 tokens (2.7$\times$ chance) and saturates near 500 tokens.
481	The monotonic increase reflects the statistical nature of the features: longer passages yield more stable estimates of perplexity percentiles and surprisal variance, reducing within-class overlap at adjacent depth boundaries.
482	
483	\subsection{Mixed-Depth Corpus}
484	\label{app:mixed_depth}
485	
486	Per-passage classification is largely independent of corpus composition: evaluating on a corpus containing mixed proportions of d0--d3 passages (rather than balanced classes) yields macro-recall $\Delta{=}{-}1.62$\,pp relative to the balanced benchmark (within the 2\,pp tolerance).
487	Individual class deltas range from $-$0.45\,pp to $-$2.43\,pp (d1 shows the largest shift), confirming that the RF classifier operates on per-sample features without sensitivity to the class distribution of the evaluation corpus.
488	Macro-recall $\Delta$ = $-$1.62\,pp (within the 2\,pp threshold); individual class variations (max 2.43\,pp on d1) arise from held-out sample composition rather than classifier sensitivity to corpus distribution, as the Random Forest produces identical per-sample predictions regardless of batch context.
489	
490	\subsection{Extended Discussion}
491	\label{app:extended_discussion}
492	
493	\paragraph{Depth-graduated curation.}
494	Current data curation operates on a binary human-vs-machine distinction \cite{dsir2023,qurating2024}.
495	Recursive generation depth estimation enables a finer-grained alternative: retain low-depth generations (d1) whose distributional properties remain close to the human source, while down-weighting or filtering high-depth text (d3+) where tail erosion has degraded quality.
496	The 70--85\% in-distribution accuracy and $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.75}{=}4$ suggest depth-graduated filtering is feasible within known model--strategy configurations, though the d1/d2 boundary inversion under cross-strategy transfer (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) means reliable curation requires either known decoding strategy metadata or an upstream strategy-identification step; a pilot study (Appendix~\ref{app:curation_poc}) yields an inconclusive RF-vs-unfiltered comparison ($p>0.4$), though RF-filtered subsets significantly outperform size-matched random subsampling ($p<0.001$--$0.02$); large-scale validation remains open.
497	Importance weighting \cite{sugiyama2007_importance_weighting} could further enable continuous depth-aware data selection rather than discrete thresholding.
498	
499	\paragraph{Toward a provenance toolkit.}
500	Depth estimation complements existing provenance methods along orthogonal axes.
501	Watermarking \cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} provides high-confidence attribution when the generator cooperates but is inapplicable to unwatermarked text.
502	Source attribution \cite{uchendu2020_attribution} identifies the generating model but not the generation history.
503	Depth estimation characterizes \emph{iterative reuse} regardless of generator cooperation or identity.
504	A layered provenance system combining these three signals (watermark presence, source model, and recursive generation depth) could support more nuanced data auditing than any single method.
505	
```

</details>

high-entropy output resists memorization, so depth-dependent distributional shifts do not accumulate.\nThis establishes \\emph{memorization as a prerequisite} for detectable depth signal: recursive depth is discriminable only when successive fine-tuning iterations progressively reshape the output distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-$\\tau$ non-transferability.}\nLeave-one-$\\tau$-out transfer yields mean accuracy of 46.7\\% with mean d1v2 AUC of 0.319 (Table~\\ref{tab:multi_tau_cross}), worse than cross-strategy transfer (59.1\\%; Train on three temperatures, test on the held-out one.}\n\\label{tab:multi_tau_cross}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc (\\%) & d1v2 AUC \\\\\n\\midrule\n$\\tau{=}0.5$ & 52.2 & 0.176 \\\\\n$\\tau{=}0.7$ & 41.0 & 0.299 \\\\\n$\\tau{=}0.9$ & 46.4 & 0.277 \\\\\n$\\tau{=}1.1$ & 47.1 & 0.525 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 46.7 & 0.319 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Feature Overlap Analysis}\n\\label{app:feature_overlap}\n\nWe probe whether end-to-end PLM representations capture the same depth signals as our statistical features via linear probing of [CLS] representations from DeBERTa-v3-base and RoBERTa-base.\nPLM representations excel at surface lexical features (TTR: $R^2{=}0.85$, repeated bigrams) but fail to capture perplexity distribution statistics; \\textsc{var\\_surprisal} and \\textsc{p90\\_ppl} yield negative $R^2$ under linear probing, indicating these features are not linearly accessible in the pretrained representation space.\nThis confirms that the 19pp accuracy gap between feature-based and end-to-end methods (\\S\\ref{app:e2e}) stems from complementary, not redundant, representations: PLMs capture lexical patterns while the targeted statistical features capture distributional tail properties that constitute the dominant depth markers.\n\n\\subsection{End-to-End Learning}\n\\label{app:e2e}\n\nDeBERTa-v3-base \\cite{he2021_deberta_v3} and RoBERTa-base \\cite{liu2019_roberta} finetuned end-to-end achieve 58.3\\% ($\\pm$4.7\\%) and 59.5\\% ($\\pm$3.2\\%) respectively, lagging the RF baseline (78.2\\%) by 19.9pp and 18.7pp.\nThe 1.2pp inter-backbone gap rules out architecture bias; Table~\\ref{tab:e2e_detail} shows both backbones share the same failure mode (high d0 F1, $>$0.94, but collapsed d1/d2 performance, F1 $\\leq$ 0.42), while RF achieves 0.596 d2 recall, a $>$2$\\times$ advantage at the hardest depth class.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Per-class performance for end-to-end PLM classifiers vs.\\ RF baseline (Pythia-1.4B nucleus). PLM columns report F1; RF d2 column reports recall (the class with the largest PLM--RF gap). Both PLM backbones share the same failure pattern: collapsed d1/d2 scores despite adequate d0/d3.}\n\\label{tab:e2e_detail}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Acc (\\%) & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nDeBERTa-v3 & 58.3\\,$\\pm$\\,4.7 & .947 & .378 & .247 & .656 \\\\\nRoBERTa-base & 59.5\\,$\\pm$\\,3.2 & .958 & .416 & .261 & .647 \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF (19 feat.) & 78.2 & --- & --- & .596$^\\dagger$ & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{@{}l}{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$Recall; shown for the depth class with the largest PLM--RF gap.}\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Feature space divergence.}\nSHAP TreeExplainer analysis confirms that perplexity and surprisal features account for 88.8\\% of RF's total contribution.\nThree independent importance measures (SHAP, Gini, and permutation) show strong concordance (Kendall's $W{=}0.90$, $p{<}0.005$; pairwise Spearman $\\rho$: SHAP--Gini $0.93$, SHAP--permutation $0.84$; \\S\\ref{app:permutation}), with \\texttt{p90\\_ppl} appearing in the SHAP top-2 in 9/9 cells and \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} in 8/9.\nLinear probing of PLM [CLS] representations yields negative $R^2$ for both \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} and \\texttt{p90\\_ppl} (\\S\\ref{app:feature_overlap}), indicating that pretrained representations lack the linear structure needed to recover perplexity tail statistics, a systematic representational gap rather than a backbone-specific artifact.\n\nDCOL \\cite{cloc2025} reached 64.5\\% (\\S\\ref{app:dcol_iterations}); all end-to-end approaches (58--65\\%) underperform feature-based methods, suggesting depth estimation benefits from targeted statistical features that capture distributional tail properties absent from PLM representations.\nThis comparison is bounded by backbone scale ($\\leq$125M parameters); whether larger PLMs (e.g., 7B+) can close the gap via richer internal representations remains an open question.\n\n\\subsection{Domain Bias Analysis}\n\\label{app:domain_bias}\n\nTo assess whether the classifier exploits depth-related features or memorizes stylistic cues from individual seed articles, we conduct three sub-experiments.\n\n\\paragraph{Methodological note.}\nAn initial implementation assigned sample IDs with a global counter across depths, inadvertently correlating seed-group identity with depth (LOGO accuracy 64.0\\%, seed-group prediction 20.8\\%).\nWe discovered the issue during internal review, corrected to per-depth seed assignment ensuring each group contains equal samples per depth, and re-ran all sub-experiments.\nThe results below use the corrected pipeline; the initial (buggy) numbers are reported for transparency.\n\n\\paragraph{Sub-A: Standard CV.}\nFive-fold CV accuracy on 3-class (d1/d2/d3) classification ranges from 59\\% to 82\\% across the 9 atlas cells (up to 2.5$\\times$ above the 33.3\\% chance level), confirming that depth signal is the dominant classification factor.\n\n\\paragraph{Sub-B: LOGO evaluation.}\nWe assign each passage to one of 10 seed-article groups (balanced: each group contains equal samples per depth) and perform leave-one-group-out CV.\nLOGO mean accuracy is 72.1\\% (range 59.5--81.6\\%), matching standard CV (72.0\\%) within noise (before fix: 64.0\\%, reflecting the depth--group confound).\nPer-cell fold standard deviations range from 0.4pp to 1.4pp, indicating that cross-group generalization is as reliable as within-group classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Sub-C: Negative control.}\nWe train two classifiers on identical features: one predicting depth, the other predicting seed-group identity (10-class, chance = 10\\%).\nDepth prediction accuracy averages 72.0\\%; seed-group prediction averages 10.2\\%, indistinguishable from chance (before fix: 20.8\\%, inflated by the depth--group confound).\nThe depth-to-seed-group accuracy ratio is 7.1$\\times$, confirming that the feature representation encodes depth but not seed-article identity.\n\nTaken together, these results show that seed-article grouping has no measurable effect on depth estimation after correcting the group assignment: LOGO accuracy equals standard CV, and seed-group prediction is at chance.\n\n\\subsection{Wild-Data Calibration}\n\\label{app:calibration}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:calibration} reports d0 accuracy and false positive rate (FPR) for all three model-specific nucleus RF classifiers on two out-of-distribution human-text corpora: WikiText-103 (546 editorial passages) and C4 (5{,}000 web-crawl passages).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Wild-data d0 accuracy and FPR for nucleus RF classifiers. All passages are human-written (true d0). FPR = fraction misclassified as d1+. 95\\% Wilson CIs computed for C4 ($n{=}5{,}000$).}\n\\label{tab:calibration}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Source & $n$ & d0 (\\%) & FPR (\\%) & 95\\% Wilson CI \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia-1.4B & WikiText & 546 & 100.0 & 0.0 & --- \\\\\n & C4 & 5{,}000 & 79.8 & 20.2 & [19.1, 21.3] \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nGPT-2~XL & WikiText & 546 & 99.3 & 0.7 & --- \\\\\n & C4 & 5{,}000 & 77.6 & 22.4 & [21.3, 23.6] \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nOLMo-1B & WikiText & 546 & 98.5 & 1.5 & --- \\\\\n & C4 & 5{,}000 & 95.3 & 4.7 & [4.2, 5.3] \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn WikiText-103, all three classifiers achieve $\\geq$98.5\\% d0 accuracy, confirming that editorial text is reliably distinguished from recursive generations across model families.\nOn C4, FPR varies sharply: OLMo (4.7\\%) vs.\\ Pythia (20.2\\%) and GPT-2~XL (22.4\\%).\nC4 misclassifications concentrate in template and SEO content with elevated repetition features (\\texttt{rep\\_3gram} $+$37\\%, \\texttt{rep\\_4gram} $+$75\\% vs.\\ correctly classified d0 passages), rather than in random noise.\nThe model gap likely reflects training data composition: OLMo (trained on Dolma, which includes diverse web data) produces d0 features more tolerant of web-crawl variation, while Pythia and GPT-2~XL (trained primarily on Wikipedia/WebText) map template-heavy content into the d1+ region.\nAmong C4 false positives for GPT-2~XL, predictions distribute as d1 (9.0\\%), d2 (3.7\\%), d3 (9.6\\%); for OLMo, d1 (2.9\\%), d2 (0.5\\%), d3 (1.3\\%). The non-uniform depth distribution suggests the classifier maps specific repetition patterns to particular depth signatures rather than producing random misclassifications.\n\n\\subsection{Downstream Curation PoC}\n\\label{app:curation_poc}\n\nTo validate that depth estimation translates to actionable data curation, we conduct a proof-of-concept downstream evaluation.\nWe fine-tune Pythia-410M with LoRA ($r{=}16$, $\\alpha{=}32$) for 3 epochs on 8 data conditions derived from the Pythia nucleus depth-estimation corpus, repeating each condition over 5 random seeds.\nConditions vary the selection mechanism (RF-predicted depth, oracle depth, random subsampling) and stringency (strict $\\approx$25\\%, moderate $\\approx$50\\%, soft threshold).\nTable~\\ref{tab:curation_poc} reports per-condition perplexity; Table~\\ref{tab:curation_comparisons} reports pairwise contrasts.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Curation PoC: per-condition perplexity (Pythia-410M LoRA, 5 seeds). $\\Delta$ is signed so that positive values indicate lower (better) perplexity than unfiltered. $^*$\\,: $p<0.05$ (paired $t$-test vs.\\ unfiltered).}\n\\label{tab:curation_poc}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nCondition & Mean PPL & Std & $n$ & $\\Delta$ vs Unfilt. & $p$ & $d_z$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nunfiltered        & 15.776 & 0.066 & 10{,}000 & ---      & ---   & ---      \\\\\nrf\\_soft          & 15.733 & 0.038 &  4{,}422 & +0.043   & 0.407 &  0.41    \\\\\nrf\\_moderate      & 15.756 & 0.056 &  4{,}940 & +0.021   & 0.492 &  0.34    \\\\\nrf\\_strict        & 15.810 & 0.061 &  2{,}496 & $-$0.034 & 0.607 & $-$0.25  \\\\\noracle\\_moderate  & 15.809 & 0.103 &  5{,}000 & $-$0.033 & 0.485 & $-$0.34  \\\\\noracle\\_strict    & 15.879 & 0.128 &  2{,}500 & $-$0.103 & 0.216 & $-$0.66  \\\\\nrandom\\_soft      & 16.062 & 0.079 &  4{,}422 & $-$0.286 & ${<}$0.001$^*$ & $-$2.77  \\\\\nrandom\\_moderate  & 15.998 & 0.090 &  4{,}940 & $-$0.222 & 0.028$^*$ & $-$1.50  \\\\\nrandom\\_strict    & 16.239 & 0.165 &  2{,}496 & $-$0.463 & 0.013$^*$ & $-$1.89  \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Pairwise comparisons (paired $t$-test, 5 seeds). $^*$\\,: $p<0.05$; $^{**}$\\,: $p<0.01$.}\n\\label{tab:curation_comparisons}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nComparison & $\\Delta$ PPL & $t$ & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nrf\\_strict vs random\\_strict     & 0.429 & $-$7.05 & 0.002$^{**}$ \\\\\nrf\\_moderate vs random\\_moderate & 0.242 & $-$3.77 & 0.020$^*$    \\\\\nrf\\_soft vs random\\_soft         & 0.328 & $-$17.98 & ${<}$0.001$^{**}$ \\\\\nrf\\_soft vs unfiltered           & 0.043 &    0.93 & 0.407        \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Reference Model Ablation}\n\\label{app:ref_ablation}\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Reference model ablation. Classification accuracy (5-fold CV) on Pythia-1.4B generated text (nucleus, d0--d3) using different reference models for feature extraction.}\n\\label{tab:ref_ablation}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\nReference Model & Architecture & Accuracy (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia-1.4B (self) & GPT-NeoX & 78.6 $\\pm$ 0.6 \\\\\nOLMo-1B (cross) & OLMo & 74.5 $\\pm$ 0.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Full Results}\n\\label{app:cross_scale_full}\n\nThis section provides the full cross-scale analysis summarized in \\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}.\nWe extend the benchmark to two 7--8B models: Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, both under 4-bit NF4 QLoRA fine-tuning, generating depths $d \\in \\{0, 1, \\ldots, 5\\}$ with nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$, 500 samples per depth).\nFeature extraction uses the same reference model (Pythia-1.4B) and 19-feature pipeline as all prior experiments.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale comparison.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:cross_scale} contrasts the two 7--8B models with the three 1--1.5B models on matched evaluation (nucleus sampling, depths 0--3).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-scale comparison: 4-class RF accuracy (\\%, depths 0--3, nucleus), $\\mathrm{EDD}_{T{=}0.70}$ (depths 0--5), and d0-vs-d1 AUC. CI: 95\\% bootstrap. Chance = 25\\%.}\n\\label{tab:cross_scale}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & 4-class acc & $\\mathrm{EDD}_{T{=}0.70}$ & d0v1 AUC \\\\\n\\midrule\nGPT-2~XL (1.5B) & 83.3 & 4 & 0.999 \\\\\nOLMo-1B & 78.8 & 4 & 0.997 \\\\\nPythia-1.4B & 78.2 & 4 & 0.998 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLlama-3.1-8B & 70.2\\,[68.3, 72.4] & 4 & 0.949 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & 60.1\\,[58.0, 62.1] & ${>}5$ & 0.969 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{(1) Depth signal persists at 7--8B scale.}\nBoth 7--8B models substantially exceed the 25\\% chance baseline: Llama-3.1-8B achieves 70.2\\% (95\\% CI $[68.3, 72.4]$) and Qwen2.5-7B achieves 60.1\\% ($[58.0, 62.1]$), confirming that recursive depth remains estimable from perplexity-based features even when the generator is 5--7$\\times$ larger than the reference model.\nDepth-0 separability is preserved in both models: d0v1 AUC is 0.949 (Llama-3.1) and 0.969 (Qwen2.5).\n\n\\paragraph{(2) EDD at 7--8B scale.}\nAt threshold $T{=}0.70$, Qwen2.5-7B maintains all consecutive-pair AUCs above 0.70 through depth~5 (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_scale_auc}), yielding $\\mathrm{EDD}_{T{=}0.70} > 5$, exceeding the $\\mathrm{EDD}{=}4$ observed at 1--1.5B.\nLlama-3.1-8B matches the smaller models at $\\mathrm{EDD}{=}4$: its monotonic decay causes d4v5 (0.618) to drop below $T$, despite stronger signal at shallower depths.\nBoth larger generators produce weaker per-step signal than the 1--1.5B models (mean consecutive AUC: 0.786 for Llama-3.1, 0.782 for Qwen2.5, vs.\\ 0.852 at 1--1.5B).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Consecutive binary AUC for 7--8B models (nucleus $p{=}0.95$). Llama-3.1-8B exhibits monotonic decay (d4v5 drops below $T{=}0.70$); Qwen2.5-7B shows a non-monotonic (U-shaped) pattern with all pairs above $T$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_scale_auc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & d0v1 & d1v2 & d2v3 & d3v4 & d4v5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLlama-3.1-8B & 0.949 & 0.877 & 0.771 & 0.716 & 0.618 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & 0.969 & 0.729 & 0.709 & 0.732 & 0.772 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{(3) Divergent AUC trajectories and instruction-tuning effect.}\nAmong instruction-tuned models, Llama-3.1-8B exhibits monotonic AUC decay consistent with the 1--1.5B pattern, while Qwen2.5-7B shows a U-shaped recovery beyond d2v3 (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_scale_auc}).\nA base-model control (Llama-3.1-8B without instruction tuning) clarifies the causal direction: the base model achieves 59.9\\% four-class accuracy (95\\% CI [57.8, 62.15], $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$) with non-monotonic AUC decay (Table~\\ref{tab:llama3_base_auc}), while the instruction-tuned variant reaches 70.2\\% with strictly monotonic decay.\nThe base-model control experiment suggests instruction-tuning regularizes rather than confounds the depth signal.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Pairwise AUC: Llama-3.1-8B Base vs.\\ Instruct.}\n\\label{tab:llama3_base_auc}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nVariant & $d_{0\\text{v}1}$ & $d_{1\\text{v}2}$ & $d_{2\\text{v}3}$ & $d_{3\\text{v}4}$ & $d_{4\\text{v}5}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase & 0.918 & 0.794 & 0.682 & 0.730 & 0.595 \\\\\nInstruct & 0.965 & 0.874 & 0.771 & 0.716 & 0.618 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe base model exhibits non-monotonic decay with a U-shaped recovery at $d_{3\\text{v}4}$, while the instruction-tuned variant shows strictly monotonic decay. Both variants exceed chance (AUC $> 0.5$) at all boundaries, confirming depth signal exists regardless of instruction-tuning, though instruction-tuning strengthens and regularizes it.\n\n\\paragraph{Caveats.}\nFor Qwen2.5-7B, the CI lower bound (58.0\\%) does not exclude sub-60\\% accuracy; Llama-3.1-8B's lower bound (68.3\\%) is more comfortably above chance but still below the 75--83\\% range at 1--1.5B.\nThe divergent AUC trajectories across two models caution against strong generalizations from only two 7--8B data points: both the monotonic (Llama-3.1) and non-monotonic (Qwen2.5) patterns are single observations, and further architectures are needed to establish which is the dominant regime at this scale.\n\n\\paragraph{Confounds.}\n\\label{app:cross_scale_confounds}\nFour confounds limit these results: (1)~4-bit NF4 QLoRA quantization (absent at 1--1.5B) may distort the perplexity distribution independently of depth; (2)~smaller sample size (500 vs.\\ 5{,}000 per depth) widens confidence intervals; (3)~evaluation is limited to nucleus sampling, leaving other strategies untested at this scale; (4)~vocabulary mismatch between 7--8B generators and the 1.4B reference model.\nThe base-model control partially addresses a former confound (instruction-tuning): since the base variant shows weaker signal with non-monotonic decay while the instruction-tuned variant shows stronger monotonic signal, instruction-tuning regularizes rather than confounds depth estimation.\nThe remaining confounds are not mutually independent (quantization and vocabulary mismatch both affect the perplexity distribution), making it infeasible to attribute the observed accuracy gap (60--70\\% vs.\\ 78--85\\% at 1--1.5B) to any single factor.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Boundary Generalization}\n\\label{app:cross_boundary}\n\nThe preceding experiments assume a single model family generates all recursive depths.\nIn practice, recursive contamination may traverse model boundaries: text generated by model~A is used to train model~B, whose output trains model~C.\nWe probe whether the depth signal persists under two relaxations of the single-model assumption.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-model recursive chains.}\nWe construct a heterogeneous chain: human text (d0) $\\to$ Pythia-1.4B generates d1 $\\to$ OLMo-1B finetuned on Pythia-d1 generates d2 $\\to$ GPT-2~XL finetuned on OLMo-d2 generates d3.\nEach recursive step uses a \\emph{different} model family, eliminating the possibility that depth signal arises from a single model's idiosyncratic behavior.\nA 4-class Random Forest achieves 84.75\\% accuracy (95\\% CI $[81.0, 88.25]$), with per-depth F1 scores of 0.98 (d0), 0.88 (d1), 0.76 (d2), and 0.76 (d3).\nPairwise AUC remains high: d0-vs-d1 = 0.999 and d2-vs-d3 = 0.841.\nThese results suggest that distributional artifacts of recursive generation persist across model boundaries, providing preliminary evidence consistent with a process-level depth signal rather than individual generator artifacts.\n\n\\paragraph{Confound discussion.}\nThe heterogeneous chain confounds architecture identity with depth (d1=Pythia, d2=OLMo, d3=GPT-2~XL).\nHowever, if the classifier exploited architecture fingerprints rather than depth signal, near-perfect accuracy would be expected since architecture perfectly determines depth label.\nThe observed 84.75\\% with monotonically decreasing per-depth F1 (d0=0.98, d1=0.88, d2=0.76, d3=0.76) is more consistent with a depth-signal interpretation, as architecture fingerprinting would not produce this graded pattern.\nWe address this confound directly with a reverse-chain control below.\n\n\\paragraph{Reverse-chain control.}\nTo empirically test the architecture confound hypothesis, we constructed a reverse chain (GPT-2~XL $\\to$ OLMo $\\to$ Pythia) reversing the model-depth assignment.\nThe reverse chain achieves 85.75\\% (95\\% CI [82.0, 89.25]), statistically indistinguishable from the forward chain (84.75\\%, [81.0, 88.25]).\nIf the classifier exploited architecture fingerprints rather than depth signal, reversing the chain should invert depth predictions; the equivalent accuracy under both orderings provides empirical evidence against the architecture confound.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-domain transfer.}\nAll prior experiments use WikiText-103 as the seed corpus.\nTo test domain dependence, we repeat the pipeline with arXiv abstracts as the seed text (Pythia-1.4B, nucleus $p{=}0.95$, 500 samples per depth, d0--d3).\nFive-fold cross-validation yields 74.1\\% $\\pm$ 2.0\\% accuracy, compared to ${\\sim}$78\\% on the WikiText seed.\nPer-depth F1 shows the familiar pattern: d0 = 1.00, d1 = 0.78, d2 = 0.52, d3 = 0.66, with d2 remaining the most challenging class.\nThe 4pp accuracy gap relative to WikiText likely reflects the narrower stylistic range of scientific abstracts, which may reduce the distributional contrast between adjacent depths.\nThese results provide preliminary evidence that the perplexity-based features capture properties of the recursive generation process rather than domain-specific artifacts of the seed corpus.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Seed Replication}\n\\label{app:seed_replication}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:seed_replication} reports within-seed and cross-seed classification accuracy for Pythia-1.4B (nucleus, depths 0--3) under two independent LoRA training seeds.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-seed transfer (Pythia-1.4B nucleus). Baseline seed=42 is the primary benchmark LoRA; seed=43 is an independent replication. $\\Delta$: difference from respective comparison point.}\n\\label{tab:seed_replication}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nCondition & Acc (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nWithin-seed (seed=42) & 78.20 & --- \\\\\nWithin-seed (seed=43) & 77.15 & $-$1.05$^\\dagger$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nCross-seed (train 42$\\to$test 43) & 77.85 & \\\\\nCross-seed (train 43$\\to$test 42) & 75.68 & \\\\\nCross-seed avg & 76.76 & $-$1.30$^\\ddagger$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$vs.\\ baseline (78.20\\%). $^\\ddagger$vs.\\ within-seed avg (78.07\\%).}\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe seed=43 LoRA achieves 77.15\\% (95\\% CI [76.4, 78.15]), within 1.05\\,pp of the seed=42 baseline.\nCross-seed transfer averages 76.76\\%, a 1.30\\,pp deficit relative to within-seed performance.\nTop-5 feature importance is identical across seeds (\\texttt{var\\_surprisal}, \\texttt{p90\\_ppl}, \\texttt{mean\\_ppl}, \\texttt{var\\_ppl}, \\texttt{p75\\_ppl}), confirming that depth signal arises from the recursive process itself rather than idiosyncratic LoRA weight configurations.\n\n\\subsection{Generation Length Ablation}\n\\label{app:length_ablation}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:length_ablation} reports classification accuracy as a function of passage length (Pythia-1.4B nucleus, depths 0--3).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Generation length ablation (Pythia-1.4B nucleus, 5-fold CV). Accuracy increases monotonically with passage length; the 9.47\\,pp spread confirms that longer passages provide stronger statistical signal.}\n\\label{tab:length_ablation}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lc@{}}\n\\toprule\nPassage length (tokens) & Acc (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\n128 & 68.35 \\\\\n256 & 73.80 \\\\\n512 & 77.82 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDepth signal is detectable from 128 tokens (2.7$\\times$ chance) and saturates near 500 tokens.\nThe monotonic increase reflects the statistical nature of the features: longer passages yield more stable estimates of perplexity percentiles and surprisal variance, reducing within-class overlap at adjacent depth boundaries.\n\n\\subsection{Mixed-Depth Corpus}\n\\label{app:mixed_depth}\n\nPer-passage classification is largely independent of corpus composition: evaluating on a corpus containing mixed proportions of d0--d3 passages (rather than balanced classes) yields macro-recall $\\Delta{=}{-}1.62$\\,pp relative to the balanced benchmark (within the 2\\,pp tolerance).\nIndividual class deltas range from $-$0.45\\,pp to $-$2.43\\,pp (d1 shows the largest shift), confirming that the RF classifier operates on per-sample features without sensitivity to the class distribution of the evaluation corpus.\nMacro-recall $\\Delta$ = $-$1.62\\,pp (within the 2\\,pp threshold); individual class variations (max 2.43\\,pp on d1) arise from held-out sample composition rather than classifier sensitivity to corpus distribution, as the Random Forest produces identical per-sample predictions regardless of batch context.\n\n\\subsection{Extended Discussion}\n\\label{app:extended_discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Depth-graduated curation.}\nCurrent data curation operates on a binary human-vs-machine distinction \\cite{dsir2023,qurating2024}.\nRecursive generation depth estimation enables a finer-grained alternative: retain low-depth generations (d1) whose distributional properties remain close to the human source, while down-weighting or filtering high-depth text (d3+) where tail erosion has degraded quality.\nThe 70--85\\% in-distribution accuracy and $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.75}{=}4$ suggest depth-graduated filtering is feasible within known model--strategy configurations, though the d1/d2 boundary inversion under cross-strategy transfer (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) means reliable curation requires either known decoding strategy metadata or an upstream strategy-identification step; a pilot study (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) yields an inconclusive RF-vs-unfiltered comparison ($p>0.4$), though RF-filtered subsets significantly outperform size-matched random subsampling ($p<0.001$--$0.02$); large-scale validation remains open.\nImportance weighting \\cite{sugiyama2007_importance_weighting} could further enable continuous depth-aware data selection rather than discrete thresholding.\n\n\\paragraph{Toward a provenance toolkit.}\nDepth estimation complements existing provenance methods along orthogonal axes.\nWatermarking \\cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} provides high-confidence attribution when the generator cooperates but is inapplicable to unwatermarked text.\nSource attribution \\cite{uchendu2020_attribution} identifies the generating model but not the generation history.\nDepth estimation characterizes \\emph{iterative reuse} regardless of generator cooperation or identity.\nA layered provenance system combining these three signals (watermark presence, source model, and recursive generation depth) could support more nuanced data auditing than any single method.\n",
```json
      "numLines": 505,
      "startLine": 1,

    }
```

```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	\paragraph{Cross-strategy deployment constraint.}
5	Cross-strategy transfer induces a d1/d2 boundary inversion under temperature sampling.
6	Per-strategy z-score normalization partially corrects the inversion under logistic regression (GPT-2~XL d1v2 AUC $0.24{\to}0.56$, OLMo $0.19{\to}0.70$) but yields negligible improvement for the Random Forest classifier used throughout the main experiments ($\Delta < 0.02$\,pp), and even in the best case (logistic regression) leaves a residual gap relative to within-strategy performance (${\sim}0.83$), indicating that a two-stage pipeline (strategy identification + strategy-conditioned classification) is needed for reliable cross-strategy deployment.
7	
8	\paragraph{d0 inflation of headline metrics.}
9	Depth~0 (human-written text) is near-perfectly separable (recall $>$98\% across all nine model--strategy settings), inflating headline accuracy.
10	Cross-model transfer accuracy of 66.4\% is substantially driven by d0; d2 recall drops to 26.8\% in the worst case (Pythia holdout).
11	
12	\paragraph{Controlled vs.\ ecological setting.}
13	The primary benchmark uses Wikipedia seed text with controlled recursive generation (LoRA fine-tuning, 1--1.5B parameter models); cross-scale experiments extend to 7--8B instruction-tuned models (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}).
14	This protocol isolates the recursive depth variable but diverges from wild recursive contamination, where models are pretrained on mixed corpora containing prior-generation outputs at unknown proportions; the fingerprints we identify may attenuate or shift under such diffuse exposure.
15	Depth signal is the dominant classification factor (5-fold CV 59--82\%, up to 2.5$\times$ chance): LOGO accuracy (72.1\%) matches standard CV (72.0\%), and seed-group prediction is at chance (10.2\% vs.\ 10\%; Appendix~\ref{app:domain_bias}).
16	Cross-scale experiments include both instruction-tuned and base variants; the base model shows weaker but above-chance depth signal (59.9\% vs.\ 70.2\%) with non-monotonic AUC decay, suggesting instruction-tuning regularizes degradation patterns.
17	Real-world documents may also interleave segments at different recursive generation depths; extending to segment-level estimation introduces a granularity--statistical-power tradeoff.
18	
19	\paragraph{Domain-dependent false positive rate.}
20	On editorial text (WikiText-103), all classifiers achieve $\geq$98.5\% d0 accuracy, but on web-crawl data (C4, 5{,}000 passages), FPR ranges from 4.7\% (OLMo) to 22.4\% (GPT-2~XL).
21	Misclassifications concentrate in template/SEO content with elevated repetition features (\texttt{rep\_3gram} $+$37\%, \texttt{rep\_4gram} $+$75\% vs.\ correctly classified d0); the model gap likely reflects training data composition (OLMo's Dolma includes diverse web data vs.\ Pythia/GPT-2~XL's Wikipedia/WebText).
22	Deployment on heterogeneous web corpora requires model- and domain-specific calibration.
23	
24	\paragraph{Adversarial robustness.}
25	Adversarial paraphrasing \cite{sadasivan2023_paraphrase} could disrupt the perplexity tail features that drive classification; depth estimation's robustness under such attacks is untested.
26	
27	\paragraph{EDD monotonicity assumption.}
28	Our EDD definition (Eq.~\ref{eq:edd}) requires all pairwise AUCs up to depth $d^*$ to exceed $T$, which is well-behaved when AUC decays monotonically, as observed in all our configurations.
29	However, \citet{bakshi2026_collapse_improvement} show that early recursion can temporarily \emph{improve} model quality, which could produce non-monotonic AUC profiles (e.g., a dip at d1/d2 followed by recovery at d2/d3).
30	Our cross-strategy transfer results provide an empirical example: training on nucleus + top-$k$ and testing on temperature yields d1-vs-d2 AUC as low as 0.129 (GPT-2~XL) and 0.253 (OLMo), violating the monotonicity assumption.
31	In such cases EDD would conservatively report a lower value than the deepest discriminable pair, potentially underestimating the true discriminable range; extending EDD to handle non-contiguous discriminable regions is future work.
32	The choice of threshold $T$ is also application-dependent: at $T{=}0.80$, several cells lose one depth level relative to $T{=}0.75$.
33	
34	\paragraph{Reference model dependency.}
35	Reference model choice affects classification accuracy by up to 11\,pp; same-family references yield optimal performance, though all configurations exceed $2.5\times$ chance.
36	Surprisal-based features are computed relative to Pythia-1.4B, which coincides with one of the three generators.
37	For the Pythia pipeline this constitutes self-model feature extraction, while OLMo and GPT-2~XL measurements are cross-model.
38	Empirically, the self-model configuration does not yield the highest accuracy (Pythia 78.2\% vs.\ GPT-2~XL 85.4\%), suggesting the overlap does not confer a systematic advantage.
39	An ablation with OLMo-1B and GPT-2 XL as alternative references shows accuracy ranging from 67--79\% across three reference architectures (Appendix~\ref{app:ref_ablation}), all exceeding $2.5\times$ chance; same-family reference yields optimal performance ($\Delta{\leq}$11\,pp).
40	The choice of reference model affects feature values; a different reference model may shift absolute feature magnitudes and potentially alter discriminability at depth boundaries.
41	Reference-free surface features alone achieve 53.7\% (confirming partial signal in lexical statistics) but 25pp below the full pipeline, underscoring that surprisal-based features are essential.
42	Simple multi-reference ensembling does not outperform the best single reference (73.7\% vs.\ 78.6\%).
43	
44	\paragraph{Single LoRA instance.}
45	Independent replication with seed=43 yields 77.15\% (95\% CI [76.4, 78.15], $\Delta{=}{-}1.05$\,pp vs.\ baseline); cross-seed transfer retains 76.76\% ($\Delta{=}{-}1.30$\,pp vs.\ within-seed avg), confirming depth signal is inherent to the recursive process rather than specific LoRA weights (Appendix~\ref{app:seed_replication}).
46	Top-5 feature importance is identical across seeds.
47	
48	\paragraph{Additional caveats.}
49	Depth signal scales with passage length (128 tokens: 68.4\%, 512 tokens: 77.8\%; Appendix~\ref{app:length_ablation}), saturating near 500 tokens; shorter passages remain above chance but with reduced discriminability.
50	Detection features degrade under domain shift \cite{pudasaini2026_detection_fails}; our depth features are likely susceptible.
51	\citet{falahati2026_curated} show that curated synthetic data need not trigger collapse, attenuating the fingerprints we exploit.
52	
53	\section*{Ethics Statement}
54	
55	This work uses publicly available pretrained language models (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, GPT-2~XL) and Wikipedia text as seed data.
56	No human subjects were involved, no private or sensitive data were collected, and no new models were released.
57	Our method is intended to support data provenance and training-data auditing; we acknowledge that depth estimation tools could in principle be misused to circumvent content moderation, though such risks are shared with all detection-oriented research.
58	Code and data will be released upon acceptance.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_018xHPTrpPUxUsteKj4UwUKd`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model Collapse Theory.}
5	\citet{shumailov2024_nature} demonstrate that recursive training on model outputs erodes distributional tails; \citet{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails} formalize this via scaling laws \cite{seddik2024synthetic}, \citet{dohmatob2025_strong_collapse} establish strong collapse guarantees, and the MAD framework \cite{alemohammad2024_mad} extends the analysis to self-consuming models.
6	Subsequent work addresses mitigation \cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate,drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse,yi2026_escaping_collapse,falahati2026_curated,wenzek2020_ccnet}, non-monotonic dynamics \cite{bakshi2026_collapse_improvement}, convergence \cite{marchi2024_heat_death,wang2026_contamination,knowledge_collapse2025,dohmatob2024_demystified}, and diversity decline \cite{guo2024curious,briesch2024selfconsuming,padmakumar2024diversity,sigma2026_spectral,synthetic_eggs2026}.
7	\citet{schaeffer2025} argue that ``model collapse'' conflates multiple distinct phenomena, underscoring the need for operationally precise metrics.
8	Dohmatob et al.\ predict that recursive training erodes distributional tails as a theoretical scaling law \citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; we operationalize this prediction into a deployable ordinal classifier, revealing strategy-dependent trajectories and non-monotonic 7B behavior that the theory does not predict.
9	All assume depth is known a priori; we address the inverse problem of estimating depth from text alone.
10	
11	\paragraph{AI Text Detection.}
12	The detection literature \cite{wu2025_detection_survey} spans statistical methods (DetectGPT \cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt}, Fast-DetectGPT \cite{fast_detectgpt2024}, Binoculars \cite{binoculars2024}, DivEye \cite{diveye2026}, GLTR \cite{gehrmann2019_gltr}, Ghostbuster \cite{verma2024_ghostbuster}, DNA-GPT \cite{yang2024_dnagpt}) and watermarking \cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark}, with known limits from paraphrasing \cite{sadasivan2023_paraphrase}, distribution shift \cite{pudasaini2026_detection_fails}, and generator barriers \cite{pu2026_generator_barrier}.
13	M4-SemEval \cite{wang2024_m4_semeval} benchmarks multilingual detection.
14	All these methods perform binary classification, discarding ordinal depth information; our approach outputs ordinal depth labels, complementing rather than replacing binary detection.
15	
16	\paragraph{Adjacent Tasks.}
17	EditLens \cite{editlens2026} estimates editing extent but does not model recursive chains; \citet{uchendu2020_attribution} classify \emph{which} model generated text; LOKI \cite{loki2025} benchmarks synthetic data detection across modalities.
18	\citet{mohamed2025_broken_telephone} study iterative generation as a ``broken telephone'' effect, characterizing \emph{how} text degrades without classifying which iteration a passage comes from.
19	Our task performs the inverse, ordinal depth estimation from text alone, discriminating between adjacent depths rather than merely detecting aggregate degradation.
20	
21	\paragraph{Ordinal Classification.}
22	Ordinal classification has a long history \cite{mccullagh1980_ordinal}: \citet{frank2001} reduce ordinal problems to nested binary tasks, and \citet{rennie2005} introduce loss functions tailored to discrete ordered labels.
23	CORAL \cite{coral2020} enforces rank consistency via ordered binary tasks; CLOC \cite{cloc2025} extends contrastive learning with multi-margin ordinal losses.
24	We adopt both as baselines (\S\ref{sec:generalization}) to confirm the signal is accessible to principled ordinal methods.
25	
26	\paragraph{Decoding Strategy Effects.}
27	Decoding strategies shape text statistics in ways that interact with depth signals \cite{holtzman2020_nucleus,finlayson2024_closing,garces_arias2025_decoding_decoded,wiher2022_decoding,basu2021_mirostat}.
28	Our Depth Degradation Atlas (\S\ref{sec:atlas}) reveals that these strategies induce qualitatively different degradation trajectories under recursive generation: temperature sampling accelerates convergence to a low-entropy attractor while top-$k$ attenuates the depth signal, effects beyond single-pass text quality.
```

</details>

CLOC \\cite{cloc2025} extends contrastive learning with multi-margin ordinal losses.\nWe adopt both as baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}) to confirm the signal is accessible to principled ordinal methods.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding Strategy Effects.}\nDecoding strategies shape text statistics in ways that interact with depth signals \\cite{holtzman2020_nucleus,finlayson2024_closing,garces_arias2025_decoding_decoded,wiher2022_decoding,basu2021_mirostat}.\nOur Depth Degradation Atlas (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas}) reveals that these strategies induce qualitatively different degradation trajectories under recursive generation: temperature sampling accelerates convergence to a low-entropy attractor while top-$k$ attenuates the depth signal, effects beyond single-pass text quality.",

Estimating Recursive Generation Depth via Statistical Fingerprints\n**Venue**: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR May 2026 cycle)\n\n---\n\n### ⚠️ 重要说明：Paper Summary 与实际论文不一致\n\nDirector 的 paper summary 描述了以下内容，但当前 tex 文件中 **不存在**：\n- **14B 实验** (Qwen2.5-14B, FP16, 76.2%)：实际论文仅有 7B（Qwen2.5-7B: 60.1%, Llama-3.1-8B: 70.2%）\n- **Prompted generation** (92.8% retention)：论文未包含此实验，BOS-seeded 生成被标注为 limitation\n- **Two-stage pipeline** (§5.4, 75.2%)：论文中 §5.4 是 \"Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations\"，无 two-stage pipeline\n- **Wild-data calibration on RedPajama-v2/Dolma**：论文使用 WikiText-103 和 C4，在 Appendix §A.5 中\n- **论文标题**：实际为 \"How Deep Is Your Data?\"，而非 Director summary 中的 \"Ordinal Depth Estimation...\"\n\n以下审稿基于 **实际 tex 文件内容**。如果 Director 描述的是计划中的修改，请确认后我可以对新版重新审稿。\n\n---\n\n## (1) Strengths\n\n**S1. 问题定义清晰且有真实空白。** Recursive depth estimation as ordinal classification（§3.1）是 binary detection 的自然且重要的扩展。Definition 1 (EDD) 数学上严谨：contiguous discriminability prefix + threshold sensitivity 是 well-motivated 的设计选择。\n\n**S2. Atlas 设计系统性优秀。** 3×3 grid + depth extension to d5 + EDD threshold sensitivity at T ∈ {0.65, 0.70, 0.75, 0.80}（Table 4）提供了全面的 landscape view。Strategy-dependent trajectory 发现（temperature 加速 convergence、top-k 衰减信号；§4.5）超越了 Dohmatob 2024 的理论预测。\n\n**S3. 多轮修订后 Related Work 显著改善。** 现在引用 Schaeffer et al. 2025（反驳 collapse 必然性）、McCullagh 1980（ordinal 经典）、Frank 2001、Rennie & Srebro 2005、CCNet（Wenzek et al. 2020）。Dohmatob 定位清晰：\"we operationalize this prediction into a deployable ordinal classifier\"（related_work.tex line 8）。\n\n**S4. Feature importance 一致性令人信服。** p90_ppl top-2 in 9/9 cells（Gini）、var_surprisal 8/9（SHAP），三种 importance measures concordance W=0.90 (p<0.005)。Top-5 features 达到 97.6% full accuracy。VIF 分析 + forward selection 提供了独立验证。\n\n**S5. 诚实的 Limitations。** 8 段 Limitations 覆盖 cross-strategy failure、d0 inflation、ecological gap、FPR variability、adversarial robustness、EDD monotonicity、reference dependency、single LoRA。Discussion 现在明确说 curation pilot \"inconclusive\"（discussion.tex line 7）。\n\n**S6. LoRA rank ablation 新增。** generalization.tex line 110-111 报告 rank 4-64 accuracy spread 4.65pp，解决了此前遗留的方法学缺失。\n\n**S7. 三个 reference model ablation。** 从单一 OLMo ablation 扩展到三个 reference architectures（67-79% range），conclusion.tex line 35-39 详细讨论。\n\n---\n\n## (2) Weaknesses\n\n### 按维度组织\n\n#### A. Task Formalization (§3.1)\n\n**W1** [minor] EDD 仍未显式声明为 operational measure。Definition 1 指定了具体的 classifier（RF 500 trees, ⌊√19⌋ features per split），但文本未明确说 \"EDD is conditioned on the classifier and feature set; a different classifier may yield different EDD values.\" §5.1 的 classifier convergence（≤0.4pp）间接表明 classifier 依赖性小，但应 explicit。\n\n#### B. Benchmark Construction (§3.2)\n\n**W2** [moderate] BOS-seeded generation 是显著生态效度限制。论文承认 \"BOS-seeded generation may amplify depth signal relative to prompted settings\"（method.tex line 35）但未提供任何 prompted generation 数据点。这对读者评估 real-world applicability 至关重要——prompted text 通常有更高的 coherence 和更低的 repetition，可能大幅衰减 depth signal。Director summary 提到 92.8% retention 实验但论文中不存在。\n\n**W3** [minor] 全量 fine-tuning 未对比。LoRA rank ablation (4-64) 是好的补充，但 LoRA 与 full fine-tuning 的 depth signal 差异未测试。在 wild 中，recursive contamination 更可能通过 full pretraining 而非 LoRA 发生。\n\n#### C. Feature Extraction (§3.3)\n\n**W4** [minor] Reference model circularity 已充分讨论但未完全解决。三个 reference models 都是 ~1-1.5B autoregressive decoder-only。Limitations 提到 \"architecturally distinct references... remains open\"，这是合理的 scope limitation。Reference-free surface features 53.7%（confirming partial signal without reference model）是有价值的 baseline。\n\n#### D. Classification (§3.4 / §5.1)\n\n**W5** [moderate] Table 2 和 Table 5 仍缺 std/CI。这是多轮审稿以来的 persistent issue。5-fold CV 天然产生 fold-level variance；Figure 4 有 bootstrap CI，cross-scale table 有 95% CI brackets，Table 9 (Appendix curation) 有 std——但两个最核心的 main-text tables 仍然只报告点估计。至少应在 Table 2 加 ±std 列。\n\n**W6** [minor] DeBERTa/RoBERTa end-to-end baselines 仅使用 base-size (~125M)。论文承认 \"whether larger PLMs (e.g., 7B+) can close the gap... remains an open question\"（section_appendix.tex line 188），但 ~125M 的 neural baseline 对 \"depth signal is inaccessible to PLM representations\" 的 claim 支撑不够强。\n\n#### E. Scale & Ecological Validity (§5.4)\n\n**W7** [moderate] Cross-scale 仅有两个 7B 模型，且均为 4-bit QLoRA。Director summary 描述了 14B FP16 实验但不在论文中。当前仅有 Qwen2.5-7B (60.1%) 和 Llama-3.1-8B (70.2%)：\n- 60.1% 的 lower CI bound (58.0%) 仅 marginally above chance at d1-d3 level\n- 两个模型 divergent AUC trajectories（monotonic vs U-shaped）无法判断哪个是 dominant pattern\n- Quantization confound 无法与 vocabulary mismatch 解耦\n- 论文正确标注为 \"Preliminary\"——这是诚实的，但削弱了 scale robustness claim\n\n**W8** [moderate] Wild-data calibration 缺乏 ground truth。Appendix §A.5 的 C4 FPR 分析假设所有 C4 passages 为 human-written (d0)，但 2023+ web crawl 数据可能已包含 LLM-generated text，使 \"false positive\" rate 成为 upper bound estimate。论文未讨论这一 caveat。\n\n#### F. Two-Stage Pipeline\n\n**W9** [info] 论文中不存在。Limitations 提到 \"a two-stage pipeline (strategy identification + strategy-conditioned classification) is needed for reliable cross-strategy deployment\"（conclusion.tex line 6）但未实现。\n\n#### G. Wild-Data Analysis\n\n**W10** [minor] Wild-data 结果仅在 Appendix，且无时间维度分析。论文使用 WikiText-103 和 C4 而非 Director summary 中的 RedPajama-v2/Dolma。GPT-2 XL 的 22.4% C4 FPR 是 deployment-critical 的数字但仅在 Appendix 中报告。\n\n#### H. Reproducibility\n\n**W11** [minor] Code/data \"will be released upon acceptance\"（conclusion.tex line 58）。当前版本无法验证 pipeline 可复现性。LoRA hyperparameters 完整（r=16, α=32, lr=2e-4, batch=8, 3 epochs, dropout=0.05），RF params 完整（200/500 trees, 5-fold CV）。总体 specification level 良好。\n\n### 独立于维度的问题\n\n**W12** [moderate] Accumulation/mixing 场景完全未测试。Appendix §A.9 \"Mixed-Depth Corpus\" 仅测试 evaluation set class balance 变化的影响（macro-recall Δ=-1.62pp），不是真正的 accumulation 场景（如 Gerstgrasser et al. 2024 的 D_k = α·D_0 + (1-α)·D_{k-1}）。Limitations 承认 \"wild recursive contamination, where models are pretrained on mixed corpora... the fingerprints we identify may attenuate or shift\"——但缺少任何实验验证。这是 ecological validity 的核心缺口。\n\n**W13** [moderate] Depth estimation vs quality estimation confound 未控制。论文未测试：在匹配 text quality（如按 perplexity bin 分层）的条件下，depth 是否仍可区分。间接证据存在（τ=1.1 signal collapse 说明不纯粹是 quality；cross-model chains 排除单一模型的 quality gradient），但无直接 confound-control 实验。\n\n**W14** [minor] Abstract \"70-85%\" 仍然将 69.6% 向上取整为 70%（Table 2 实际 lower bound）。\n\n---\n\n## (3) Questions for Authors\n\nQ1. LoRA rank ablation（§5.4 末尾）仅提到 \"accuracy spread 4.65pp\"。能否提供 per-rank accuracy 数据（r=4, 16, 64）和对应的 feature importance rankings？rank 对 EDD 有影响吗？\n\nQ2. Cross-scale 中 Qwen2.5-7B 的 U-shaped AUC 恢复（d2v3=0.709 → d3v4=0.732 → d4v5=0.772）是否可能反映 instruction-tuning data 在 d3+ 深度引入了训练数据中已有的 pattern？base Qwen2.5-7B 的 AUC trajectory 是否也是 U-shaped？\n\nQ3. GPT-2 XL 的 d3v4 AUC 恰好等于 threshold 0.750（p=0.505）。如果使用 one-sided test H₀: AUC ≤ 0.75，这是 null result。你们是否考虑过在 EDD 定义中引入 statistical significance 要求（如 AUC > T AND p < α）？\n\nQ4. C4 FPR 分析假设所有 passages 为 human-written。考虑到 C4 的时间范围，这一假设在 2023+ 数据上是否仍然合理？是否有子集分析（如按 document date 分层）？\n\nQ5. Curation PoC（Appendix §A.6）中 RF-filtered vs unfiltered 无显著差异（p=0.407）。这是否因为 Pythia-1.4B 在 depth 0-3 数据上的 depth signal 本身太弱以至于过滤不产生质量差异？还是因为 downstream task（perplexity evaluation on Pythia-410M）对 training data quality 不够敏感？\n\n---\n\n## (4) Preliminary Scores\n\n| Dimension | Score | Justification |\n|-----------|-------|---------------|\n| **Soundness** | 3.5/5 | 核心方法论正确：EDD 定义严谨、feature importance 通过三种 measures 验证、domain bias control 充分。但 Table 2/5 缺 CI（persistent issue）、accumulation 和 quality-control 实验缺失削弱 ecological validity claims。GPT-2 XL EDD=4 at p=0.505 是统计 boundary case。 |\n| **Excitement** | 3.5/5 | Ordinal depth estimation 是真正的文献空白。Strategy-dependent trajectories 和 \"memorization as prerequisite\" insight（Appendix §A.4: τ=1.1 collapse）是有趣的 empirical finding。但 practical applicability 受限于 (a) strategy-conditioned 需求未实现、(b) curation PoC inconclusive、(c) wild-data FPR 高达 22.4%。 |\n| **Overall** | 3.5/5 | 论文相比前轮有实质性改善：Related Work 补全、LoRA rank ablation 新增、Introduction 措辞修正、Discussion curation narrative 诚实化、Abstract 精简。核心贡献（task formalization + atlas + EDD）solid for Findings。主要残余问题：(1) Tables 2/5 缺 CI 是 long-standing gap；(2) accumulation scenario 是 ecological validity 的 hard gap；(3) cross-scale 仅两个 7B+QLoRA 模型（Director summary 描述的 14B FP16 实验不在当前稿件中）。若 Director summary 中的计划内容（14B、prompted、two-stage pipeline、RedPajama/Dolma）能实际纳入论文，Overall 可升至 4.0。 |\n\n**Confidence**: 4/5 — 对 method/experiments 维度有高置信度；对 Director summary 中描述但不在 tex 中的内容无法评判。\n\n---\n\n### 相比 Round 8 的变化追踪\n\n| Issue | Round 8 状态 | 当前状态 | 变化 |\n|-------|-------------|---------|------|\n| R5-W4 (Related Work 遗漏) | ❌ NOT ADDRESSED | ✅ RESOLVED | 全部补齐 |\n| R1-W3 (LoRA rank) | ❌ NOT ADDRESSED | ✅ RESOLVED | rank 4-64 ablation |\n| R2-N2 (novelty positioning) | ⚠️ | ✅ RESOLVED | Dohmatob explicit positioning |\n| R2-N3 (Abstract overload) | ⚠️ | ✅ RESOLVED | 精简，无内部引用 |\n| R2-W1 (universal overclaim) | ⚠️ | ✅ RESOLVED | 改为 \"consistent in direction but scale-dependent\" |\n| R2-W2 (curation \"enables\") | ⚠️ | ✅ RESOLVED | 改为 \"inconclusive\" |\n| R1-N2 (Table CI) | ⚠️ PARTIAL | ⚠️ STILL PARTIAL | Table 2/5 仍缺 |\n| R4-N1 (EDD stat qualification) | ⚠️ PARTIAL | ✅ IMPROVED | Abstract 更简洁，详情在正文 |\n| R7-W3 (accumulation) | ❌ NOT ADDRESSED | ❌ STILL MISSING | |\n| R7-W4 (quality control) | ❌ NOT ADDRESSED | ❌ STILL MISSING | |\n| R5-W5 (EDD operational) | ⚠️ PARTIAL | ⚠️ STILL PARTIAL | |\n\n**总结**：论文在 Related Work、novelty positioning、Abstract 质量、LoRA ablation、reference model ablation、Discussion 诚实性上有实质进步。核心残余：(1) Tables 缺 {
```json
  "text": "## Round 9 交叉回应 — Reviewer 1 对 Reviewer 2 修订版审稿\n\n---\n\n### Must-Fix\n\n**[AGREE] R2-N1: Reference model 消融表格与正文 claim 不一致**\n\n完全同意，这是 blocking issue。我独立验证了以下事实：\n\n1. **method.tex line 41**: \"Ablations with OLMo-1B **and GPT-2 XL** as alternative references yield **67--79%** accuracy\"\n2. **conclusion.tex line 39**: \"accuracy ranging from **67--79%** across **three** reference architectures\"\n3. **Table 16 (tab:ref_ablation, section_appendix.tex lines 298-310)**: 仅有 **2 行**数据——Pythia-1.4B (78.6±0.6%) 和 OLMo-1B (74.5±0.7%)。**GPT-2 XL 作为 reference model 的数据完全缺失。**\n\n这是直接的 claim-evidence 矛盾：\n- \"67%\" 的下界必须来自 GPT-2 XL reference 数据，但该数据不在表中\n- \"three reference architectures\" 与表中 2 行数据不符\n- Introduction contribution bullet 3 也提到 \"reference model robustness (67--79% accuracy across three reference models)\"\n\n这不是措辞问题而是 **数据缺失**。可能的情况：(a) GPT-2 XL reference 实验已完成但结果遗漏入表；(b) 实验未完成但文字 claim 先行写入。无论哪种，作为审稿人我无法验证 \"67%\" 这一数字的真实性。\n\n**建议**：必须在 Table 16 补入 GPT-2 XL reference 数据行，或将所有正文 claim 修改为仅覆盖现有两个 reference models。这应在提交前立即修复。\n\n---\n\n### Should-Fix\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W1: \"signal universality\" overclaim 仍在 generalization.tex line 108**\n\n确认。原文：\"Cross-model recursive chains (84.75%)... further confirm signal universality.\" 这是从 Round 8 以来的遗留问题。虽然 Introduction 已将 \"universal\" 修正为 \"consistent in direction but scale-dependent in magnitude\"，但 generalization.tex 中此处遗漏了同步修改。\n\nCross-strategy transfer 仅 59.1%（d1-d3: 45.9%），cross-τ 仅 46.7%。\"Signal universality\" 在这些数据下仍是 overclaim。建议改为 \"signal robustness across model boundaries\" 或 \"cross-model signal persistence\"。\n\n---\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W2: Abstract \"70-85%\" 向上取整**\n\n同意。我在 Round 8 也标记了此问题（当时的 N1）。Table 2 实际范围 69.6%--85.4%，将 69.6% 取整为 70% 仍未修复。建议改为 \"approximately 70--85%\" 或 \"69.6--85.4%\"。问题不大但容易修复。\n\n---\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W3: Contribution bullet 3 信息过载**\n\n同意。Introduction contribution bullet 3（introduction.tex lines 28-29）在一个 bullet 中堆叠 7 个 generalization axes + 对应数字，读起来像 results summary。这是从 Round 8 R2-W4 以来的遗留问题。建议拆分为 (a) 列出 axes 名称，(b) 将数字移至正文各节。\n\n---\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W4: 跨策略迁移实用性瓶颈讨论不足**\n\n同意，并认为这是比 R2 评估更重要的问题。\n\n核心数据：\n- Cross-strategy d1-d3 accuracy: 45.9%（chance=33%, 仅 1.38×）\n- Temperature holdout d1v2 AUC 低至 0.129（GPT-2 XL, §5.3 Table 7）\n- Cross-τ LOO transfer: 46.7%（Appendix §A.4）\n\nLimitations 仅提到 \"a two-stage pipeline... is needed\" 但未实现。这是论文声称的核心应用场景（\"depth-graduated data curation\"）的实际部署瓶颈：在真实 web data 中，decoding strategy 通常未知。如果不知道 strategy，分类器可能产生 inverted depth predictions（d1v2 AUC < 0.5），这比不做分类更糟糕。\n\n我在 Round 1 审稿中也标记了此问题（W9: two-stage pipeline 不存在于论文中）。建议至少在 Limitations 中更强调这一实际部署障碍，或实现一个 minimal two-stage pipeline proof-of-concept。\n\n---\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W5: 2 个 orphan bib 条目**\n\n确认。我独立验证：\n- `c2pa2024` (C2PA Technical Specification): 在 references.bib line 495 但未在任何 .tex 文件中被 `\\cite`\n- `chen2024spin` (Self-Play Fine-Tuning): 在 references.bib line 549 但未在任何 .tex 文件中被 `\\cite`\n\n两者均为 orphan entries。可能是修订过程中加入 bib 但未完成正文引用。如果打算引用（如 C2PA 用于 provenance toolkit 讨论），需在正文添加 `\\cite`；否则从 .bib 删除以避免 ACL camera-ready 检查工具报错。\n\n---\n\n### R2 新发现\n\n**[AGREE] RAID benchmark 未引用**\n\n同意。Dugan et al. 2024 \"RAID: A Shared Benchmark for Robust Evaluation of Machine-Generated Text Detectors\" (ACL 2024) 是 AI text detection 的主要 benchmark。论文 Related Work §2 \"AI Text Detection\" 段引用了 M4-SemEval 和 LOKI 作为 benchmark，但遗漏了 RAID。考虑到 RAID 的影响力，应补充引用。\n\n**[AGREE] Abstract 括号内列举不完整**\n\n同意。Abstract line 6 写 \"including cross-scale with 7B models at 70.2%, cross-domain, and LoRA-rank ablation\"，仅列举 3/7 axes。建议要么列出所有 7 个 axis 名称（不带数字），要么改为 \"including cross-model, cross-scale, and cross-domain transfer\"（选最重要的 3 个即可，但当前选择的 3 个不是最具代表性的——cross-model transfer 比 LoRA-rank ablation 更重要但未列出）。\n\n**[REBUT] Curation PoC threshold sensitivity 缺失**\n\n部分反驳。R2 指出 curation PoC 缺 threshold sensitivity 分析。但考虑到 (a) PoC 本身已报告为 inconclusive（Discussion line 7），(b) RF-filtered vs unfiltered p=0.407 已是 null result，在一个已经 inconclusive 的 PoC 上要求 threshold sensitivity 分析投入产出比不高。如果作者选择扩展 curation 实验（如使用更大 downstream model 或更敏感的 downstream task），那么 threshold sensitivity 会变得有意义。但在当前 null-result 状态下，额外分析不会改变结论。\n\n---\n\n### 总结\n\nR2 的 5 条 Should-Fix 和 1 条 Must-Fix 中，我 **5/6 完全同意**，1 条（Curation threshold sensitivity）部分反驳。\n\n**R2-N1 是 blocking issue**——这是数据与 claim 的直接矛盾，不是措辞问题。Table 16 必须补入 GPT-2 XL reference 数据或修改所有 \"three reference architectures\" / \"67-79%\" 的正文声明。\n\n我在 Round 1 审稿中标记的 W12（accumulation scenario）和 W13（quality-control confound）是 R2 未覆盖的独立方法学问题，仍然有效。",
```
